#### Image Studio

##### Public definition

Image Studio is the full-screen editor that opens from any Image Studio - whether it was created by Text to Image, Edit Image, Blend Images, or Photo Shoot. It gives you two ways to work with the images after the creation is made: AI-prompted editing and upscaling - plus a gallery view for working across multiple images at once, and a Details panel showing provenance information for each image.

##### Customer problem this solves

Businesses need more visual content than they can realistically photograph, commission, retouch, and repurpose by hand.

##### Why this feature is valuable

This feature is valuable because it reduces manual handoffs around customer jobs such as: refine a created image without starting over, upscale for print or large-format use, create variations of a strong result. It also matters as part of a larger system: the source, conversation, or finished output can remain connected to the broader IdeoLab project instead of being treated as an isolated one-off task.

##### Why this matters in the IdeoLab experience

General image tools can generate and edit excellent images. IdeoLab’s stronger marketing story is not simply image quality; it is that visual creation sits inside a broader business-content system where research, references, source images, social workflows, graphics, sites, and automation can contribute to the same project. At the feature level, emphasize the workflow around the asset - source context, review, refinement, reuse, publishing, or analytics - not unsupported claims that the raw AI output is universally better.

##### Detailed use-case library

###### Use case 1: Refining a created image without starting over

A creation came back close to what was needed, but one element is off - a background that doesn’t fit, a colour that clashes, an object that shouldn’t be there. Rather than starting over, open Image Studio, select the image, and describe the specific change you want. The revised version stays with the rest of the work so you can compare and continue refining it.

###### Use case 2: Upscaling for print or large-format use

An image created for social media needs to be used in a printed brochure or a large-format banner. The original resolution won’t hold at print size. Select the image in Image Studio, switch to Upscale mode, and apply 4× - the resolution quadruples and the upscaled version lands in the Studio alongside the original.

###### Use case 3: Creating variations of a strong result

One image in a creation stands out. Select it in Image Studio, enter the Edit Image mode, and send the same prompt again - with or without a small variation in the instruction - to create additional variations of the winning direction without touching the other images in the Studio.

###### Use case 4: Copying the original prompt to reuse it

The prompt that produced a creation needs to be reused - either to create more at a higher Variations count, or to adapt it for a related but different image. Open Image Studio, select the image, open the Details panel, and click the copy button next to the prompt. Paste it back into the chat composer to create from.

- First draft is not the finish line. Studio refinement makes AI imagery an iterative production workflow.

## 7. AI Social Content

### System-level marketing definition

IdeoLab creates social content from multiple kinds of source material - an idea, a webpage, real photos, or a video - then gives the user a dedicated Studio to review, edit, publish, schedule, and learn from performance.

### System-level customer problem

The hardest part of social media for many businesses is not opening a scheduler. It is continuously turning what the business already knows and owns into enough relevant, on-brand content to keep publishing.

### System-level differentiation

Buffer, Sprout, Hootsuite, and Canva now combine AI, publishing, and analytics to varying degrees. IdeoLab should differentiate on source-to-content workflows, connected business context, reuse of real owned assets, cross-format creation, and the feedback loop between content intelligence and what gets created next.

### Feature index

- Topic-to-Social Posts
- Webpage-to-Social Posts
- Photos-to-Social Posts
- Video-to-Social Posts
- Social Studio: Review, Edit, Publish & Schedule
- Social Analytics & AI Learning Loop

#### Topic-to-Social Posts

##### Public definition

Topic is the most open-ended Social Skill: describe any theme, idea, or message and the AI creates a batch of social media posts around it. No URL, photos, or video needed - just a description of what the posts should be about. Every Send produces a Social Studio holding the created posts, with per-platform previews for Instagram, Facebook, LinkedIn, and Twitter.

##### Customer problem this solves

The hardest part of social media for many businesses is not opening a scheduler. It is continuously turning what the business already knows and owns into enough relevant, on-brand content to keep publishing.

##### Why this feature is valuable

This feature is valuable because it reduces manual handoffs around customer jobs such as: build a brand awareness content series, create posts for a product or service launch, produce educational content for thought leadership. It also matters as part of a larger system: the source, conversation, or finished output can remain connected to the broader IdeoLab project instead of being treated as an isolated one-off task.

##### Why this matters in the IdeoLab experience

Buffer, Sprout, Hootsuite, and Canva now combine AI, publishing, and analytics to varying degrees. IdeoLab should differentiate on source-to-content workflows, connected business context, reuse of real owned assets, cross-format creation, and the feedback loop between content intelligence and what gets created next. At the feature level, emphasize the workflow around the asset - source context, review, refinement, reuse, publishing, or analytics - not unsupported claims that the raw AI output is universally better.

##### Detailed use-case library

###### Use case 1: Building a brand awareness content series

Situation. A brand manager, marketing director, or founder wants to establish a consistent brand presence on social media. They have a clear brand voice and message but need a steady stream of posts that communicate what the brand stands for. What they want to bring to life. A batch of brand-aligned posts that communicate the brand’s values, personality, and positioning - varied enough to feel fresh across a week but consistent enough to reinforce the brand identity. The problem this feature solves. Brand awareness posting needs to be consistent and frequent, which means writing posts week after week. Maintaining quality and voice while producing volume is time-consuming, and it’s easy for individual posts to drift from the brand voice when written in a hurry. What it creates. A batch of brand-aligned social posts created from the brand voice description and key messages - varied in angle and format across the batch (questions, statements, stories, CTAs) but consistent in tone and positioning. The opportunity. The brand maintains a consistent social presence week after week without a social media manager spending hours on copywriting. The Creative Brief step lets the team approve the AI’s interpretation of the brand voice before any posts are created. This creates the same batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 2: Creating posts for a product or service launch

Situation. A business owner, product marketer, or startup founder is launching a new product, feature, or service. They need a coordinated set of social posts for the launch - building anticipation, announcing the launch, and driving first-week conversion. What they want to bring to life. A launch content batch that covers the different moments - the teaser, the announcement, the feature highlight, the social proof hook, the CTA - each formatted for different platforms. The problem this feature solves. Writing a launch content batch from scratch requires creativity, copywriting skill, and a strategic understanding of launch sequencing. Most teams either scramble with low-quality copy or hire a specialist for the launch window. What it creates. A batch of launch posts created from the product/feature description and launch goals - announcement-style, benefit-led, curiosity-driving, and CTA-focused posts spread across the requested count. The opportunity. The team walks into launch week with a full content batch ready to schedule. The Creative Brief step lets them confirm the AI understood the key product differentiators before posts are created. This creates the batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 3: Producing educational content for thought leadership

Situation. A consultant, coach, agency principal, or subject-matter expert wants to build a thought leadership presence on LinkedIn or Twitter. They know their topic area deeply but don’t have time to write educational posts regularly. What they want to bring to life. A batch of educational posts that demonstrate expertise - sharing insights, explaining concepts, debunking myths, or walking through processes - written in a tone that positions the author as a credible authority. The problem this feature solves. Writing educational content that genuinely teaches something and positions the author as an expert requires both domain knowledge and copywriting skill. Producing it at volume requires time most subject-matter experts don’t have. What it creates. A batch of educational posts created from the topic area and the specific angle - tips, explainers, myth-busters, or frameworks - in the voice and tone that best positions the author for their target audience. The opportunity. The expert can maintain a consistent thought leadership presence without spending time on copywriting each week. The Creative Brief step ensures the AI understood the specific angle and audience before creating anything. This creates the same batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 4: Creating a seasonal or holiday campaign

Situation. A retailer, restaurant, service business, or brand needs to produce a campaign around a seasonal moment - a holiday, a cultural event, a season change - with posts that feel timely, on-brand, and ready to schedule. What they want to bring to life. A batch of seasonal posts that acknowledge the moment, connect it to the brand’s offer or message, and drive the right action - from a discount announcement to a seasonal menu to a gift guide. The problem this feature solves. Seasonal campaigns arrive on predictable dates, but producing quality content in advance requires planning. Most businesses scramble in the final week before a holiday because writing from scratch is slow, and the window to post is short. What it creates. A batch of seasonal posts created from the brand context and seasonal moment - balancing the celebratory tone of the season with the brand’s messaging and any promotional offer that applies. The opportunity. The brand has seasonal content ready to schedule weeks in advance. The Creative Brief step lets the team confirm the tone and angle before posts are created. Multiple seasonal batches can be produced in a single session. This creates each seasonal batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 5: Filling a content calendar for a month

Situation. A marketing manager, content strategist, or business owner has a monthly content calendar with planned themes - product features, values, tips, promotions, community - but the calendar is empty and publishing is in two weeks. What they want to bring to life. A full month’s worth of social content covering all the planned themes, balanced across formats and platforms, ready to drop into the scheduling tool. The problem this feature solves. Filling a content calendar requires producing a high volume of varied content in a short time. Writing each post individually takes hours; agencies charge for volume at a rate that stresses the budget. What it creates. Multiple batches - one per theme - created from each topic described in the prompt, at whatever post count fills the calendar. Each batch comes out in under two minutes, and the whole calendar can be populated in a single session. The opportunity. The marketing team ends a single working session with a fully populated content calendar ready for review and scheduling. The Creative Brief step per batch lets them confirm each theme’s interpretation before committing to creation. This produces the same volume in under 10 minutes of creation time.

###### Use case 6: Driving community engagement through questions and stories

Situation. A community manager, brand, or creator wants to increase engagement on their posts - comments, shares, reactions - by publishing content that invites the audience to participate rather than just consuming. What they want to bring to life. A batch of engagement-first posts - questions, fill-in-the-blank, “share your story” prompts, opinion challenges - that start conversations and signal to platform algorithms that the account drives interaction. The problem this feature solves. Engagement content requires a different writing approach than broadcast content - it needs to be open-ended, relatable, and emotionally triggering enough to make the audience want to respond. Most brands default to promotional content and wonder why their engagement is low. What it creates. A batch of community-focused posts created from the brand or community context - questions, reaction prompts, polls, or story starters - at the post count that fills the engagement content slot in the calendar. The opportunity. The account’s engagement metrics improve as the community responds to content designed to generate responses. Platform algorithms surface content with high engagement, increasing organic reach. This creates the batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 7: Promoting an upcoming event or webinar

Situation. An event organiser, business, or creator is running an event - a webinar, a conference, a workshop, a live session - and needs social content to drive registrations in the weeks leading up to it. What they want to bring to life. A promotional content batch covering the event run-up: the save-the-date, the speaker spotlight, the benefit-focused announcement, the last-chance reminder, and the day-of post - each with a clear CTA to register or attend. The problem this feature solves. Event promotion requires sustained content output over several weeks - awareness posts early, urgency posts close to the date. Most event organisers produce one or two posts and wonder why registrations are low. What it creates. A batch of event promotion posts created from the event details and registration goal - sequenced to build anticipation, highlight value, and drive registrations - in the formats best suited to the primary platform. The opportunity. The event has a full promotional content plan without the organiser writing each post individually. Creating a fresh batch for each phase of the run-up takes under two minutes per phase. This creates each phase’s content in under two minutes.

###### Use case 8: Maintaining a consistent posting cadence during a busy period

Situation. A business owner, solo creator, or small marketing team needs to keep posting consistently during a period when other priorities dominate - a product launch, a busy trading period, a team crunch. The social feed can’t go quiet, but there’s no time to write content. What they want to bring to life. Enough posts to fill the calendar for the next 2-4 weeks, covering the brand’s usual topics, so the feed stays active and the audience sees consistent output regardless of what’s happening internally. The problem this feature solves. Social media consistency requires time that disappears exactly when the business is busiest. The feed going quiet is visible to followers and algorithms; gaps in posting reduce reach and credibility. What it creates. A large batch of posts - 20, 30, or 45 - created from the brand’s usual topic mix in a single send, covering enough content to keep the calendar full through the busy period. The opportunity. The business or creator enters the busy period with a full content buffer already scheduled. Social presence is maintained without requiring daily writing time. This creates the batch in under two minutes and leaves only review and scheduling.

- Building a brand awareness content series. Lead with: Brand awareness posting needs to be consistent and frequent, which means writing posts week after week. Payoff: The brand maintains a consistent social presence week after week without a social media manager spending hours on copywriting.
- Creating posts for a product or service launch. Lead with: Writing a launch content batch from scratch requires creativity, copywriting skill, and a strategic understanding of launch sequencing. Payoff: The team walks into launch week with a full content batch ready to schedule.

Lead with: Engagement content requires a different writing approach than broadcast content - it needs to be open-ended, relatable, and emotionally triggering enough to make the audience want to respond. Payoff: The account’s engagement metrics improve as the community responds to content designed to generate responses.
- Promoting an upcoming event or webinar. Lead with: Event promotion requires sustained content output over several weeks - awareness posts early, urgency posts close to the date. Payoff: The event has a full promotional content plan without the organiser writing each post individually.
- Maintaining a consistent posting cadence during a busy period. Lead with: Social media consistency requires time that disappears exactly when the business is busiest. Payoff: The business or creator enters the busy period with a full content buffer already scheduled.
- The content bottleneck is upstream of scheduling. Businesses often need help creating enough relevant content before a scheduler matters.
- Use the real business as the content source. Photos, webpages, and videos can become posts instead of sitting unused.
- Create → publish → measure → improve. Social should become a learning system, not a one-way posting machine.

#### Webpage-to-Social Posts

##### Public definition

Page URL lets you paste any public webpage and create a batch of social media posts from its content. The AI reads the page - a blog post, a product page, a press release, a landing page - and turns it into platform-ready posts. Every Send produces a Social Studio holding the created posts, with per-platform previews for Instagram, Facebook, LinkedIn, and Twitter.

##### Customer problem this solves

The hardest part of social media for many businesses is not opening a scheduler. It is continuously turning what the business already knows and owns into enough relevant, on-brand content to keep publishing.

##### Why this feature is valuable

This feature is valuable because it reduces manual handoffs around customer jobs such as: promote a new blog post on social media, drive traffic to a product page, repurpose a press release as social content. It also matters as part of a larger system: the source, conversation, or finished output can remain connected to the broader IdeoLab project instead of being treated as an isolated one-off task.

##### Why this matters in the IdeoLab experience

Buffer, Sprout, Hootsuite, and Canva now combine AI, publishing, and analytics to varying degrees. IdeoLab should differentiate on source-to-content workflows, connected business context, reuse of real owned assets, cross-format creation, and the feedback loop between content intelligence and what gets created next. At the feature level, emphasize the workflow around the asset - source context, review, refinement, reuse, publishing, or analytics - not unsupported claims that the raw AI output is universally better.

##### Detailed use-case library

###### Use case 1: Promoting a new blog post on social media

Situation. A content marketer, blogger, or marketing team just published a new blog post. They need to promote it on social media - but writing a different social angle for each platform from scratch duplicates effort and is often skipped or rushed. What they want to bring to life. A batch of social posts that draw directly from the blog post’s content - quotes, key insights, statistics, or angles - written in a social-native style for each platform, each with a link back to the post. The problem this feature solves. Blog promotion is an afterthought for most content teams. The post gets written, published, and one generic “check out our new post” tweet goes out. The content’s actual value - the insights, the data, the story - never reaches social audiences because adapting it is time-consuming. What it creates. A batch of social posts created from the blog post URL - pulling the most shareable angles, quotes, and takeaways from the actual article content - formatted for each platform in styles that perform natively rather than looking like cross-posts. The opportunity. Every blog post gets its own social promotion package, created at publish time, ready to be scheduled across the week. The Creative Brief step lets the content team confirm which angles the AI pulled before posts are created. ##### Use case 2: Driving traffic to a product page

Situation. An ecommerce brand, SaaS company, or service business wants to drive traffic to a specific product or service page - a new arrival, a feature announcement, a popular service. They need posts that capture the page’s value proposition and turn platform audiences into page visitors. What they want to bring to life. A batch of product-focused posts that highlight the key benefits, features, or offer on the product page - with a strong CTA that drives clicks back to the page. The problem this feature solves. Product pages are written for conversion on the page itself. Extracting social-native angles from them - benefit-led headlines, curiosity hooks, social proof angles - requires a different writing approach that most product teams don’t have time for. What it creates. A batch of product-focused posts created from the page URL - pulling the headline benefits, key features, and offer details from the page and rewriting them for social-native formats - with link CTAs to the product page. The opportunity. Every product or service page has a social promotion campaign created from it in minutes. The Creative Brief step confirms which benefits the AI identified as most shareable before creation. This creates the same batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 3: Repurposing a press release as social content

Situation. A PR manager, communications team, or business owner just issued a press release. The news in it - a new partnership, a funding round, a product launch, an award - is worth sharing on social media, but press release language doesn’t work on social platforms. What they want to bring to life. A social-native batch of posts based on the press release’s news - rewritten in conversational, engaging language for each platform, without the formal register of a press release. The problem this feature solves. Press releases are written for journalists, not social audiences. Translating the same news into social-ready language requires a separate writing pass. Most companies either skip it or post a bland “read our press release” link. What it creates. A batch of announcement posts created from the press release URL - extracting the news, the key facts, and the significance - rewritten in social-native styles appropriate for LinkedIn (professional), Twitter (punchy), Instagram (visual-narrative), and Facebook (conversational). The opportunity. News that might otherwise reach only journalists now reaches the company’s social audience in formats that feel native to each platform. The Creative Brief step lets the PR team confirm the key message framing before creation. ##### Use case 4: Creating social proof content from a case study

Situation. A B2B company, agency, or service provider has published a customer case study on their website. The case study demonstrates real results - but most prospects never read it. Social posts that summarise the story and results could drive far more awareness of the proof point. What they want to bring to life. A batch of social posts built from the case study - highlighting the customer’s challenge, the solution, and the measurable result - in formats that generate engagement and credibility for the brand. The problem this feature solves. Case studies are long, formal documents. Extracting the story’s highlights for social requires editorial work - identifying the most compelling angle, finding the quotable moments, distilling the result into a punchy format. Most marketing teams produce one LinkedIn post from a case study and leave the rest of the value untapped. What it creates. A batch of social proof posts created from the case study URL - narrative-led posts that tell the customer’s story in 3-4 sentences, statistics-led posts that lead with the headline result, and quote-style posts from key moments in the case study. The opportunity. Every case study becomes a social proof content machine. The Creative Brief step lets the team confirm the story angle the AI identified as most compelling before creation. ##### Use case 5: Promoting a webinar or event landing page

Situation. A company, creator, or community is running a webinar, workshop, or event. They’ve set up a registration or event landing page. They need promotional social posts that drive registrations from their social audience. What they want to bring to life. A batch of event promotion posts drawn from the event landing page - summarising the topic, the speakers, the date, and the value proposition - with registration CTAs that link back to the page. The problem this feature solves. Event landing pages contain all the information social posts need - topic, speakers, date, registration link - but reformatting that into social-native posts requires manual extraction. Most event organisers post once or twice and miss the full registration potential. What it creates. A batch of event promotion posts created from the landing page URL - benefit-led posts, speaker-spotlight posts, last-chance-reminder posts - all pulling from the actual page content with link CTAs to the registration page. The opportunity. The event has a full promotion batch created from the landing page in minutes, ready to be distributed across the promotional window. Multiple batches can be created for different phases (initial announcement, mid-run, last chance). ##### Use case 6: Amplifying a featured news article or media mention

Situation. A business, founder, or brand has been featured in a news article, podcast transcript, or media piece. They want to share that recognition on social media without just posting a raw link - they need posts that frame the recognition and encourage clicks. What they want to bring to life. A batch of social posts that frame the media mention as a credibility moment - highlighting the key quote, the angle of the article, or the recognition the brand received - with a link back to the piece. The problem this feature solves. Sharing a media mention well requires framing it for social audiences rather than just linking to it. Most brands either post a dry “we were featured in X” link or skip sharing it entirely because crafting the right angle takes time they don’t have in the moment. What it creates. A batch of social posts created from the article URL - pulling the most credibility-building quotes, the headline angle, and the recognition framing - in formats that feel natural for each platform. The opportunity. Media mentions become compound assets - they generate both the original coverage and a social campaign that extends the coverage to the brand’s own audience. The Creative Brief step lets the brand confirm the angle before posts are created. ##### Use case 7: Creating posts from a competitor or market announcement

Situation. A marketer, analyst, or founder wants to create commentary or reaction posts around a major industry announcement - a competitor launch, an industry report, or a market development. The source URL is the announcement; the posts frame the brand’s perspective on it. What they want to bring to life. A batch of posts that use the announcement as a jumping-off point - commentary from the brand’s perspective, insight posts that add context, or opinion pieces that position the brand as informed and relevant. The problem this feature solves. Reacting to industry news in real time on social requires fast writing. Most brands either react with a generic “interesting news” comment or don’t react at all because crafting thoughtful commentary from scratch takes time. What it creates. A batch of commentary posts created from the announcement URL - pulling the key facts and framing them from the brand’s perspective, as specified in the prompt instructions. The opportunity. The brand joins relevant industry conversations quickly, with posts that feel informed and substantive rather than generic. The Creative Brief step lets the team confirm the angle and tone before creation. ##### Use case 8: Creating teaser content for a pricing or offer page

Situation. A business is running a promotion, a limited-time offer, or a new pricing tier. The offer details are on a landing page. They need social posts that communicate the offer compellingly and drive urgency. What they want to bring to life. A batch of offer-focused posts drawn directly from the landing page - highlighting the key savings, the value, the deadline, and the CTA - in formats that create urgency and drive clicks. The problem this feature solves. Offer pages are written to convert visitors who land there. Extracting the offer into social-native language - urgency-building, benefit-first, deadline-aware - requires a separate writing pass that takes time the team doesn’t have during a campaign launch. What it creates. A batch of offer-focused posts created from the landing page URL - benefit-led posts, urgency posts, deadline-reminder posts - all pulling from the actual offer details on the page, with CTAs that link back. The opportunity. Every promotional campaign has a social content batch created in minutes at launch time. Multiple batches can be created for different moments in the promotional window (launch, mid-run, last chance). This creates the full package in under two minutes per phase.

##### Marketing angle library

- Promoting a new blog post on social media. Lead with: Blog promotion is an afterthought for most content teams. Payoff: Every blog post gets its own social promotion package, created at publish time, ready to be scheduled across the week.
- Driving traffic to a product page. Lead with: Product pages are written for conversion on the page itself. Payoff: Every product or service page has a social promotion campaign created from it in minutes.
- Repurposing a press release as social content. Lead with: Press releases are written for journalists, not social audiences. Payoff: News that might otherwise reach only journalists now reaches the company’s social audience in formats that feel native to each platform.

- Promoting a webinar or event landing page. Lead with: Event landing pages contain all the information social posts need - topic, speakers, date, registration link - but reformatting that into social-native posts requires manual extraction. Payoff: The event has a full promotion batch created from the landing page in minutes, ready to be distributed across the promotional window.
- Amplifying a featured news article or media mention. Lead with: Sharing a media mention well requires framing it for social audiences rather than just linking to it. Payoff: Media mentions become compound assets - they generate both the original coverage and a social campaign that extends the coverage to the brand’s own audience.
- Creating posts from a competitor or market announcement. Lead with: Reacting to industry news in real time on social requires fast writing. Payoff: The brand joins relevant industry conversations quickly, with posts that feel informed and substantive rather than generic.
- Creating teaser content for a pricing or offer page. Lead with: Offer pages are written to convert visitors who land there. Payoff: Every promotional campaign has a social content batch created in minutes at launch time.
- The content bottleneck is upstream of scheduling. Businesses often need help creating enough relevant content before a scheduler matters.
- Use the real business as the content source. Photos, webpages, and videos can become posts instead of sitting unused.
- Create → publish → measure → improve. Social should become a learning system, not a one-way posting machine.

#### Photos-to-Social Posts

##### Public definition

Photos turns a batch of uploaded images into a set of social media posts - one post per photo. Upload your photos, describe the mood or theme, and the AI writes a caption and hashtags for each image. Every Send produces a Social Studio holding the created posts, with per-platform previews for Instagram, Facebook, LinkedIn, and Twitter.

##### Customer problem this solves

The hardest part of social media for many businesses is not opening a scheduler. It is continuously turning what the business already knows and owns into enough relevant, on-brand content to keep publishing.

##### Why this feature is valuable

This feature is valuable because it reduces manual handoffs around customer jobs such as: create social posts from a product photography shoot, turn event photos into a social recap series, publish behind-the-scenes content. It also matters as part of a larger system: the source, conversation, or finished output can remain connected to the broader IdeoLab project instead of being treated as an isolated one-off task.

##### Why this matters in the IdeoLab experience

Buffer, Sprout, Hootsuite, and Canva now combine AI, publishing, and analytics to varying degrees. IdeoLab should differentiate on source-to-content workflows, connected business context, reuse of real owned assets, cross-format creation, and the feedback loop between content intelligence and what gets created next. At the feature level, emphasize the workflow around the asset - source context, review, refinement, reuse, publishing, or analytics - not unsupported claims that the raw AI output is universally better.

##### Detailed use-case library

###### Use case 1: Creating social posts from a product photography shoot

Situation. An ecommerce brand, product-based business, or creative studio has just completed a product photography session. They have 10-30 high-quality product shots and need captions, hashtags, and posts ready for Instagram, Facebook, and Pinterest. What they want to bring to life. One social-ready post per photo - each with a caption tailored to the product and the brand voice, hashtags appropriate for the product category, and a consistent tone across the whole batch. The problem this feature solves. Writing captions for a full product photography batch is repetitive and time-consuming. Even experienced social media managers take 2-3 minutes per post, which means a 20-photo batch takes nearly an hour before scheduling. What it creates. A post per photo - captions written from the product context described in the prompt, hashtags created for the product category and brand, and all posts ready to review and schedule in Social Studio. The opportunity. A photography session that previously produced unlabelled files now produces a complete social content batch in minutes. The Creative Brief step lets the brand confirm the caption tone and approach before creation. This creates the full batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 2: Turning event photos into a social recap series

Situation. A business, community, or creator has just hosted or attended an event - a conference, a networking session, a product launch, a team day out. The event photographer delivered 20-40 photos. They want to post a recap series across LinkedIn, Instagram, and Facebook. What they want to bring to life. A social recap series - one post per event photo - that narrates the event’s highlights, energy, and key moments in a way that feels authentic to followers who weren’t there. The problem this feature solves. Event recap posting is time-sensitive (post while the event is fresh) but caption writing is slow. Most event organisers either post photos without context or write a single collage post that barely tells the story. What it creates. A batch of event recap posts - one per photo - with captions that narrate the moment shown in each image, written in the tone and voice described in the prompt. Hashtags and event tags are included based on the prompt. The opportunity. Event coverage reaches the brand’s social audience at full impact, with each photo given a story. The full batch is created while the event is still fresh, ready to schedule across the week. ##### Use case 3: Publishing behind-the-scenes content

Situation. A brand, creator, or team wants to build audience trust and relatability by sharing behind-the-scenes content - office life, the production process, team moments, a day in the life. They have a set of candid photos from a recent week or project. What they want to bring to life. A batch of behind-the-scenes posts that feel authentic and personal - casual, storytelling captions that give followers a window into how the brand or team works. The problem this feature solves. Behind-the-scenes content is effective for engagement but hard to caption well. Each photo needs a story that feels genuine rather than corporate - and writing that tone at scale takes editorial skill and time. What it creates. A batch of behind-the-scenes posts with storytelling captions written in the casual, authentic tone described in the prompt - each one adding context and personality to the photo it accompanies. The opportunity. The brand’s human side is consistently visible on social without the team spending time writing individual captions. The Creative Brief step confirms the tone before creation so all captions feel consistent. ##### Use case 4: Creating content from food or hospitality photography

Situation. A restaurant, café, catering company, or food brand has professional or smartphone photos of dishes, drinks, or the venue. They need Instagram-ready posts with captions that evoke appetite and atmosphere. What they want to bring to life. A batch of food-focused posts with evocative captions - descriptive language that makes the dish or drink sound irresistible - plus relevant hashtags for the food niche and a CTA to visit or order. The problem this feature solves. Food photography deserves captions that match the visual quality - sensory language, atmosphere, an invitation. Writing this well is a specialist skill. Most restaurants default to generic “come taste our food” copy that doesn’t land. What it creates. A batch of food and venue posts with sensory, appetite-triggering captions created from the restaurant or food context described in the prompt - matched to each photo individually. The opportunity. The restaurant’s social feed looks and reads like a premium food destination rather than a template. The Creative Brief step lets the owner confirm the tone and audience before creation. This creates the full batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 5: Showcasing team members or employees on social

Situation. An employer brand manager, HR team, or business owner wants to humanise the brand by featuring team members on social. They have headshots or candid team photos and want posts that highlight each person’s role and personality. What they want to bring to life. A team spotlight post series - one post per team member photo - with warm, genuine captions that introduce the person and their role, and feel consistent in tone across the batch. The problem this feature solves. Team spotlight posts are a high-value employer branding tool but require individual writing for each person. Without a consistent template, they often feel unbalanced - some are detailed, others rushed. What it creates. A batch of team spotlight posts - one per photo - with captions that introduce each person based on the context provided in the prompt, in a consistent tone that feels genuine rather than templated. The opportunity. The brand’s employer presence on LinkedIn and Instagram improves consistently, with team members featured regularly without the HR or marketing team spending time on individual captions. ##### Use case 6: Turning before-and-after transformation photos into posts

Situation. A home renovation business, interior designer, personal trainer, beauty professional, or any business that delivers visible transformations has before-and-after photo pairs. These are among the most shareable formats on social media. What they want to bring to life. A series of transformation posts - one per before/after pair - with captions that tell the story of the transformation, highlight the process or result, and invite enquiries. The problem this feature solves. Before-and-after photos are powerful proof points but need captions that tell the story - what the challenge was, what changed, what the result means for the client. Writing this narrative well requires copywriting skill and knowledge of the transformation. What it creates. A batch of transformation story posts - one per photo - with captions created from the transformation context described in the prompt, highlighting the result and inviting the reader to achieve the same outcome. The opportunity. Every completed project becomes a social post immediately. The business’s portfolio is consistently visible on social without a marketing team writing individual project stories. This creates the full batch in under two minutes.

###### Use case 7: Creating posts from customer photo submissions

Situation. A brand, e-commerce business, or creator has collected customer photos - user-generated content from a review campaign, a hashtag challenge, or direct submissions. They want to use the photos for social proof posts. What they want to bring to life. A batch of social proof posts - one per customer photo - with captions that highlight the customer experience, the product in use, and the authenticity of the submission. Posts feel genuine, not promotional. The problem this feature solves. User-generated content is the most credible form of social proof, but curating it into posts takes time - selecting the best images, writing appropriate captions that. What it creates. A batch of social proof posts - one per customer photo - with captions written in a genuine, community-focused tone created from the brand context and the nature of the submissions described in the prompt. The opportunity. The brand’s social feed features real customers consistently, building community trust without individual caption writing. The batch can be published as a “from our community” series across the week. ##### Use case 8: Producing travel or location photography posts

Situation. A travel brand, tour operator, hotel, or destination marketing organisation has a batch of location photography. They want to post a series of destination posts that inspire travel and drive enquiries or bookings. What they want to bring to life. A batch of destination posts - one per photo - with evocative, inspiring captions that capture the feeling of the place, the experience available, and a CTA to enquire or book. The problem this feature solves. Travel copywriting requires a specific tone - evocative, aspirational, sensory - that most general copywriters find hard to sustain across a full batch. Writing 15 destination captions that all feel fresh and inspiring takes significant time and skill. What it creates. A batch of destination posts with inspiring captions created from the destination or experience described in the prompt - each one matched to the photo’s visual context, with CTAs that drive enquiry or booking. The opportunity. The destination’s social feed consistently inspires its target audience with high-quality content created from the brand’s own photography. The Creative Brief step confirms the tone and audience before creation. This creates the full batch in under two minutes.

#### Video-to-Social Posts

##### Public definition

Video turns a video you’ve added to your lab into a batch of social media posts. The AI analyses the video content - transcripts, scenes, key moments - and creates posts around it. Describe the angle you want, choose how many posts to create, and the AI writes captions and hashtags ready for Instagram, Facebook, LinkedIn, and Twitter. Every Send produces a Social Studio holding the created posts.

##### Customer problem this solves

The hardest part of social media for many businesses is not opening a scheduler. It is continuously turning what the business already knows and owns into enough relevant, on-brand content to keep publishing.

##### Why this feature is valuable

This feature is valuable because it reduces manual handoffs around customer jobs such as: create teaser posts from a long-form youtube tutorial, repurpose a podcast episode as social posts, create social posts from a product demo video. It also matters as part of a larger system: the source, conversation, or finished output can remain connected to the broader IdeoLab project instead of being treated as an isolated one-off task.

##### Why this matters in the IdeoLab experience

Buffer, Sprout, Hootsuite, and Canva now combine AI, publishing, and analytics to varying degrees. IdeoLab should differentiate on source-to-content workflows, connected business context, reuse of real owned assets, cross-format creation, and the feedback loop between content intelligence and what gets created next. At the feature level, emphasize the workflow around the asset - source context, review, refinement, reuse, publishing, or analytics - not unsupported claims that the raw AI output is universally better.

##### Detailed use-case library

###### Use case 1: Creating teaser posts from a long-form YouTube tutorial

Situation. A creator, educator, or brand has published a long-form YouTube tutorial - a how-to video, a course lesson, a product walkthrough. They want to drive views on YouTube by posting engaging teaser content on Instagram, LinkedIn, and Twitter. What they want to bring to life. A batch of teaser posts that pull the most compelling moments from the tutorial - a surprising insight, a before/after result, a key tip - and invite the audience to watch the full video. The problem this feature solves. Long-form video contains dozens of quotable, shareable moments. Identifying those moments, extracting them, and turning them into social-native posts requires watching the video, identifying timestamps, and writing distinct posts for each moment - a process that takes hours for a single video. What it creates. A batch of teaser posts created from the video content - quote-style posts, insight posts, hook-first posts - with captions that tease the video’s value and CTAs that drive viewers to the full YouTube tutorial. The opportunity. Every YouTube video published gets its own social promotion campaign created in minutes at release. The Creative Brief step lets the creator confirm the angle - which moments to tease - before posts are created. ##### Use case 2: Repurposing a podcast episode as social posts

Situation. A podcast producer, creator, or brand publishes weekly podcast episodes. They want to promote each episode on social media with posts that capture the best moments - key quotes, conversation highlights, episode themes - and drive podcast listeners. What they want to bring to life. A batch of episode promotion posts - quote cards, teaser posts, “in this episode” summaries - written in a social-native format that makes the episode’s value clear to someone who hasn’t heard it. The problem this feature solves. Podcast content is rich in shareable moments but locked inside an audio format. Extracting social-ready posts requires listening back, transcribing key moments, and writing separate posts for each angle - a process that can take 1-3 hours per episode. What it creates. A batch of episode promotion posts created from the video/recording - quote-style posts, insight posts, episode theme summaries - with CTAs that drive the audience to listen to the full episode. The opportunity. Every episode release includes a full social promotion batch created at the same time, ready to schedule across the week. Consistent promotion drives episode discoverability and subscription growth. This creates the full package in under two minutes.

###### Use case 3: Creating social posts from a product demo video

Situation. A SaaS company, app developer, or product team has produced a product demo video - a walkthrough of features, a customer use case, or a before/after workflow demonstration. They want to promote it on LinkedIn, Twitter, and Instagram. What they want to bring to life. A batch of demo-focused posts that highlight the product’s key capabilities from the video - feature-specific posts, use case posts, and result-focused posts - each driving viewers to watch the demo or start a trial. The problem this feature solves. Product demo videos contain multiple distinct value propositions that each deserve their own social post. Writing individual posts for each capability shown requires watching the demo, identifying the distinct moments, and writing social-native copy for each - a process marketing teams rarely complete. What it creates. A batch of product-focused posts created from the demo video - capability highlight posts, use case posts, result posts - with CTAs that drive demo views or product trials. The opportunity. Every product demo becomes a multi-post social campaign that drives sustained awareness across the week it’s released. The Creative Brief step lets the product marketing team confirm the capabilities highlighted before creation. This creates the full package in under two minutes.

###### Use case 4: Creating LinkedIn posts from a conference keynote

Situation. A speaker, conference organiser, or brand has a recording of a conference talk or keynote. They want to extract the key insights for a LinkedIn thought leadership campaign that drives engagement from the professional audience who missed the talk. What they want to bring to life. A batch of LinkedIn posts based on the keynote - insight posts, quote posts, “key takeaway” posts - written in a professional thought leadership tone that drives comments and shares. The problem this feature solves. Conference talks are packed with insights that rarely reach beyond the audience who was there. Extracting them for LinkedIn requires watching the recording, transcribing key moments, and rewriting them as professional posts - typically a 2-4 hour task per talk. What it creates. A batch of LinkedIn posts created from the keynote recording - insight summaries, provocative takes, quotable moments - in a professional thought leadership tone that performs on LinkedIn. The opportunity. Every conference appearance generates a sustained LinkedIn content campaign rather than a single post-event update. The speaker or brand maintains visible presence on LinkedIn in the weeks following the event. This creates the full campaign in under two minutes.

###### Use case 5: Extracting quote posts from a video interview

Situation. A media brand, content team, or creator has filmed a video interview with a subject-matter expert, a client, or a leader. The interview contains quotable moments that would perform well as social posts - but identifying and extracting them is time-consuming. What they want to bring to life. A batch of quote-style posts - each featuring a compelling statement or insight from the interviewee - formatted for Twitter, LinkedIn, or Instagram quote cards. The problem this feature solves. Interviews are long, and the best quotes are scattered throughout. Watching a 45-minute interview to pull 10 quotable moments, then writing caption context for each quote, takes 1-2 hours before any posts are scheduled. What it creates. A batch of quote posts created from the interview video - the most shareable, insightful, or surprising statements from the interviewee - formatted in social-native styles with context captions. The opportunity. Every interview becomes a social content asset that creates posts at release time without a dedicated team member watching the full recording. The Creative Brief step confirms the angle - which type of quotes to extract - before creation. ##### Use case 6: Promoting a how-to video series

Situation. A brand, creator, or educator has a library of how-to videos - tutorials, tips, guides - and wants to use each video as the source for a social promotion batch that drives views and builds authority in their topic area. What they want to bring to life. A batch of how-to teaser posts drawn from each video - tip extracts, process summaries, hook-first posts that surface the video’s value - with CTAs that drive viewers to watch the full how-to. The problem this feature solves. How-to content is highly searchable and shareable on social, but extracting the teachable moments from each video and reformatting them as social posts requires dedicated time per video - time most creators don’t have. What it creates. A batch of how-to teaser posts created from the video - tip summaries, process-step posts, “did you know” hooks - with CTAs to the full video. One batch per video, created at release time. The opportunity. The brand’s social feed consistently features actionable, educational content that drives engagement and positions the brand as an authority. Every video in the library can create a social batch. ##### Use case 7: Creating a webinar recap content series

Situation. A company, agency, or creator has run a webinar and has the recording. They want to turn the webinar’s content into a series of social posts that provide value to followers who missed the live session and drive sign-ups for future webinars. What they want to bring to life. A batch of webinar recap posts - key insight summaries, expert takeaways, “what we covered” posts - that make the webinar’s value visible on social and position the host as a source of expert content. The problem this feature solves. Webinar recordings are valuable content assets that rarely get promoted effectively after the live session. Most companies send a follow-up email with a replay link and call it done - the content’s social potential goes untapped. What it creates. A batch of webinar recap posts created from the recording - insight posts, statistic highlights, “top takeaways” summaries - in formats that perform on LinkedIn and Twitter, with CTAs that drive replay views or future event sign-ups. The opportunity. Every webinar recording creates a social content series that extends the event’s reach beyond the live attendees. The Creative Brief step confirms the key takeaways the AI identified before creation. ##### Use case 8: Turning a testimonial video into social proof posts

Situation. A business, agency, or service provider has a video testimonial from a happy client or customer. They want to use it to create social proof posts that build credibility with prospects. What they want to bring to life. A batch of social proof posts drawn from the testimonial video - quote posts, result summaries, “what our customers say” posts - that feel authentic and credible, not like traditional advertising. The problem this feature solves. Testimonial videos are powerful social proof, but most businesses post the video once and don’t extract its content further. The quotes and results inside the video - the most credible elements - never reach platform audiences as standalone posts. What it creates. A batch of social proof posts created from the testimonial video - quote extracts, result summaries, story posts - in a tone that feels genuine and credibility-building rather than promotional. The opportunity. Every testimonial video creates a social proof campaign. Posts can be scheduled across weeks, providing sustained credibility signals to prospects researching the brand. This creates the full batch in under two minutes.

Lead with: How-to content is highly searchable and shareable on social, but extracting the teachable moments from each video and reformatting them as social posts requires dedicated time per video - time most creators don’t have. #### Social Studio: Review, Edit, Publish & Schedule

##### Public definition

Social Studio is the full-screen editor that opens from any created social post batch - created by Topic, Page URL, Photos, or Video. It gives you everything you need to review, edit, and publish your posts: per-platform previews (Instagram, Facebook, LinkedIn, Twitter), a caption editor, an AI Pilot panel for post-level text and image edits, download options, and a publish flow.

##### Customer problem this solves

The hardest part of social media for many businesses is not opening a scheduler. It is continuously turning what the business already knows and owns into enough relevant, on-brand content to keep publishing.

##### Why this feature is valuable

This feature is valuable because it reduces manual handoffs around customer jobs such as: review and editing a batch before publishing, adapt a post for a specific platform, download posts for a client handoff. It also matters as part of a larger system: the source, conversation, or finished output can remain connected to the broader IdeoLab project instead of being treated as an isolated one-off task.

##### Why this matters in the IdeoLab experience

Buffer, Sprout, Hootsuite, and Canva now combine AI, publishing, and analytics to varying degrees. IdeoLab should differentiate on source-to-content workflows, connected business context, reuse of real owned assets, cross-format creation, and the feedback loop between content intelligence and what gets created next. At the feature level, emphasize the workflow around the asset - source context, review, refinement, reuse, publishing, or analytics - not unsupported claims that the raw AI output is universally better.

##### Detailed use-case library

###### Use case 1: Reviewing and editing a batch before publishing

A marketing manager creates 10 posts from a campaign theme. Before publishing, they open Social Studio to review. Two posts have captions that don’t match the brand tone - they use Edit Text in the AI Pilot to rewrite those captions with a single instruction each. One post has an image that doesn’t match the topic - they switch to Edit Image and regenerate it. The rest look perfect. They select all 10 posts and schedule them across a 5-day range.

###### Use case 2: Adapting a post for a specific platform

A LinkedIn-focused post in the batch has a caption that’s too long for Twitter. The marketing team switches to the Twitter tab in the platform preview, sees the truncation, and edits the caption for brevity while on the Twitter view. The LinkedIn caption remains unchanged. Both versions are correct for their respective platforms.

###### Use case 3: Downloading posts for a client handoff

An agency has created 20 posts for a client’s social campaign. Rather than publishing directly, they download the full ZIP - images and caption CSV - and send it to the client for review and scheduling in the client’s own social tool.

###### Use case 4: Publishing a batch to multiple platforms at once

A business owner creates 5 posts from a product page URL. Once their social accounts are connected, they can review the posts and publish the approved content across the selected platforms from the same workspace.

###### Use case 5: Scheduling a content batch across a week

A content team creates 30 posts at the start of the month. They select all 30, click Publish, and choose the Range scheduling mode - spreading the posts from the 1st to the 28th of the month. The posts are distributed evenly across the range without any further scheduling work.

- Use the real business as the content source. Photos, webpages, and videos can become posts instead of sitting unused.
- Create → publish → measure → improve. Social should become a learning system, not a one-way posting machine.

#### Social Analytics & AI Learning Loop

Important evidence note: verified product update marketing capability. Exact network/metric coverage must be verified against current product documentation before making granular claims.

##### Public definition

Social Analytics is a founder-confirmed IdeoLab capability designed to centralize social performance and let AI use what is working as input for future messaging and content decisions.

##### Customer problem this solves

The hardest part of social media for many businesses is not opening a scheduler. It is continuously turning what the business already knows and owns into enough relevant, on-brand content to keep publishing.

##### Why this feature is valuable

This feature is valuable because it reduces manual handoffs around customer jobs such as: see social performance in one place, identify what messages and content are working, use AI to turn performance insights into better future content. It also matters as part of a larger system: the source, conversation, or finished output can remain connected to the broader IdeoLab project instead of being treated as an isolated one-off task.

##### Why this matters in the IdeoLab experience

Buffer, Sprout, Hootsuite, and Canva now combine AI, publishing, and analytics to varying degrees. IdeoLab should differentiate on source-to-content workflows, connected business context, reuse of real owned assets, cross-format creation, and the feedback loop between content intelligence and what gets created next. At the feature level, emphasize the workflow around the asset - source context, review, refinement, reuse, publishing, or analytics - not unsupported claims that the raw AI output is universally better.

##### Detailed use-case library

###### Use case 1: Stop guessing what to post next

Situation. A business is publishing consistently but has no reliable way to turn performance data into the next content decision.

What it enables. IdeoLab’s analytics layer can centralize the performance picture and give AI a signal about what messages and content patterns are working.

The opportunity. The marketing story is a shift from content production as a one-way factory to a learning loop: create, measure, learn, improve, create again.

###### Use case 2: Find the message that is earning attention

Situation. A team has multiple themes, offers, and content angles in market but cannot tell which messages deserve more investment.

What it enables. AI can use performance information as input for analysis and recommend which message patterns to repeat, refine, or retire.

The opportunity. The value is strategic focus: create more of what is resonating instead of merely creating more.

###### Use case 3: Bring fragmented social reporting into one working view

Situation. A marketer is checking native platform dashboards separately and manually reconciling what happened.

What it enables. IdeoLab is intended to bring social analytics into one place so the content conversation can happen beside the performance evidence.

The opportunity. The content angle is less dashboard-hopping and faster connection between insight and creative action.

###### Use case 4: Turn analytics into a new brief

Situation. A business knows last month’s top posts but still has to manually explain those learnings to the person or AI creating next month’s content.

What it enables. The performance signal can become context for AI-assisted message refinement and future content generation.

The opportunity. The strategic story is that analytics should not end in a report; it should change the next brief.

###### Use case 5: Use performance to improve positioning

Situation. Several offers or value propositions are being tested socially, and the team wants to learn which language is most compelling.

What it enables. Social performance can be treated as market feedback that helps sharpen messaging rather than as vanity metrics only.

The opportunity. This creates a bridge between content analytics and broader marketing strategy.

###### Use case 6: Close the loop for a small team

Situation. A small business can create and publish content but lacks a dedicated analyst to review performance and translate it into recommendations.

What it enables. AI-assisted analytics can reduce the gap between measurement and action.

The opportunity. The value is not replacing judgment; it is giving a small team a repeatable way to learn from its own content.
